```
1→"""Stage-gate and coverage helpers for triage command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→from desloppify.engine.plan import TRIAGE_IDS
     7→
     8→
     9→def _require_triage_pending(plan: dict, *, action: str) -> bool:
    10→    """Require at least one triage stage ID to be present in queue for an action."""
    11→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    12→    if order & TRIAGE_IDS:
    13→        return True
    14→    print(colorize(f"  No triage stage in the queue — nothing to {action}.", "yellow"))
    15→    return False
    16→
    17→
    18→def _validate_stage_report(
    19→    report: str | None,
    20→    *,
    21→    stage: str,
    22→    min_chars: int,
    23→    missing_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    24→    short_guidance: list[str] | None = None,
    25→) -> str | None:
    26→    """Validate staged report presence/length and print consistent guidance."""
    27→    if not report:
    28→        print(colorize(f"  --report is required for --stage {stage}.", "red"))
    29→        for line in missing_guidance or []:
    30→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    31→        return None
    32→    cleaned = report.strip()
    33→    if len(cleaned) < min_chars:
    34→        print(
    35→            colorize(
    36→                f"  Report too short: {len(cleaned)} chars (minimum {min_chars}).",
    37→                "red",
    38→            )
    39→        )
    40→        for line in short_guidance or []:
    41→            print(colorize(f"  {line}", "dim"))
    42→        return None
    43→    return cleaned
    44→
    45→
    46→def _triage_coverage(plan: dict) -> tuple[int, int, dict]:
    47→    """Return (organized, total, clusters) for triage progress."""
    48→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    49→    all_cluster_ids: set[str] = set()
    50→    for cluster in clusters.values():
    51→        all_cluster_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    52→    queue_ids = [issue_id for issue_id in plan.get("queue_order", []) if issue_id not in TRIAGE_IDS]
    53→    organized = sum(1 for issue_id in queue_ids if issue_id in all_cluster_ids)
    54→    return organized, len(queue_ids), clusters
    55→
    56→
    57→def _unenriched_clusters(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, list[str]]]:
    58→    """Return clusters with issues that are missing required enrichment."""
    59→    gaps: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
    60→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    61→        if not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
    62→            continue
    63→        if cluster.get("auto"):
    64→            continue
    65→        missing: list[str] = []
    66→        if not cluster.get("description"):
    67→            missing.append("description")
    68→        if not cluster.get("action_steps"):
    69→            missing.append("action_steps")
    70→        if missing:
    71→            gaps.append((name, missing))
    72→    return gaps
    73→
    74→
    75→def _manual_clusters_with_issues(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
    76→    """Return names of non-auto clusters that have issues."""
    77→    return [
    78→        name
    79→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
    80→        if cluster.get("issue_ids") and not cluster.get("auto")
    81→    ]
    82→
```

---

## Implementation Plan: Hard Enforcement for Workflow Steps

### Problem Summary

When resolving `workflow::score-checkpoint` or `workflow::create-plan`, the code at `override_handlers.py` lines 500-523 takes the synthetic ID path which calls `purge_ids()` with zero content validation. The `--confirm` flag auto-generates attestation from any note over 50 chars, making these workflow gates trivially bypassable.

### Architecture Overview

The fix introduces a new validation module (`workflow_validation.py`) alongside the existing triage validation pattern, and integrates it into the synthetic ID resolution path in `cmd_plan_resolve()`. The key insight is that triage already has excellent enforcement patterns in `_stage_validation.py` and `confirmations.py` -- we replicate those patterns for workflow items.

---

### File 1: New file `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/workflow_validation.py`

This is the main new module. It follows the pattern of `triage/_stage_validation.py`: each workflow step gets a dedicated validation function that checks content against actual state data.

```python
"""Validation and guardrail helpers for workflow synthetic items."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID,
    WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID,
)

# --- Constants ---
_MIN_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_NOTE = 150
_MIN_CREATE_PLAN_NOTE = 200


def _get_dimension_names(state: dict) -> list[str]:
    """Extract dimension names from state dimension_scores."""
    return sorted(state.get("dimension_scores", {}).keys())


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# workflow::score-checkpoint validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def validate_score_checkpoint(
    *,
    note: str | None,
    state: dict,
    plan: dict,
    force: bool = False,
) -> str | None:
    """Validate note for score-checkpoint resolution.

    Returns None on success, error message string on failure.
    """
    if not note:
        return "workflow::score-checkpoint requires --note with score analysis."

    note_stripped = note.strip()

    # 1. Minimum length
    if len(note_stripped) < _MIN_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_NOTE:
        return (
            f"Note too short: {len(note_stripped)} chars "
            f"(minimum {_MIN_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_NOTE} for score checkpoint).\n"
            f"  Describe the current strict score, which dimensions are "
            f"weakest, and what the delta means."
        )

    # 2. Must reference the actual strict score number
    from desloppify import state as state_mod
    snapshot = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    strict = snapshot.strict
    if strict is not None:
        # Accept the score rounded to 0 or 1 decimal place
        strict_int = str(int(round(strict)))
        strict_1d = f"{strict:.1f}"
        if strict_int not in note_stripped and strict_1d not in note_stripped:
            return (
                f"Note must reference the actual strict score "
                f"({strict_1d}/100).\n"
                f"  Include the number in your analysis."
            )

    # 3. Must mention at least 1 dimension name
    dim_names = _get_dimension_names(state)
    if dim_names:
        note_lower = note_stripped.lower()
        mentioned = [
            d for d in dim_names
            if d.lower().replace("_", " ") in note_lower
            or d.lower() in note_lower
        ]
        if len(mentioned) < 1:
            dim_list = ", ".join(dim_names[:8])
            return (
                f"Note must mention at least 1 dimension name.\n"
                f"  Available dimensions: {dim_list}"
            )

    return None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# workflow::create-plan validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def validate_create_plan(
    *,
    note: str | None,
    plan: dict,
    force: bool = False,
) -> str | None:
    """Validate note for create-plan resolution.

    Returns None on success, error message string on failure.
    """
    if not note:
        return "workflow::create-plan requires --note with plan review analysis."

    note_stripped = note.strip()

    # 1. Minimum length
    if len(note_stripped) < _MIN_CREATE_PLAN_NOTE:
        return (
            f"Note too short: {len(note_stripped)} chars "
            f"(minimum {_MIN_CREATE_PLAN_NOTE} for create-plan).\n"
            f"  Describe the clusters you created, their priorities, "
            f"and the execution strategy."
        )

    # 2. Must have at least 1 manual cluster with issues
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    manual_clusters = [
        name for name, c in clusters.items()
        if c.get("issue_ids") and not c.get("auto")
    ]
    if not manual_clusters:
        return (
            "Cannot resolve workflow::create-plan: no manual clusters "
            "with issues exist.\n"
            "  Create clusters first:\n"
            '    desloppify plan cluster create <name> --description "..."\n'
            "    desloppify plan cluster add <name> <issue-patterns>"
        )

    # 3. Clusters must be enriched (have description + action_steps)
    unenriched = []
    for name in manual_clusters:
        c = clusters[name]
        missing = []
        if not c.get("description"):
            missing.append("description")
        if not c.get("action_steps"):
            missing.append("action_steps")
        if missing:
            unenriched.append((name, missing))
    if unenriched:
        lines = [
            f"Cannot resolve workflow::create-plan: "
            f"{len(unenriched)} cluster(s) need enrichment."
        ]
        for name, missing in unenriched:
            lines.append(f"    {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}")
        lines.append(
            '  Fix: desloppify plan cluster update <name> '
            '--description "..." --steps "step1" "step2"'
        )
        return "\n".join(lines)

    # 4. Note must reference at least one cluster name
    note_lower = note_stripped.lower()
    mentioned = [n for n in manual_clusters if n.lower() in note_lower]
    if not mentioned:
        cluster_list = ", ".join(manual_clusters[:8])
        return (
            f"Note must reference at least one cluster name.\n"
            f"  Your clusters: {cluster_list}"
        )

    return None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dispatch
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_WORKFLOW_VALIDATORS = {
    WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID: "score-checkpoint",
    WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID: "create-plan",
}


def validate_workflow_item(
    workflow_id: str,
    *,
    note: str | None,
    state: dict,
    plan: dict,
    force: bool = False,
) -> str | None:
    """Validate a workflow item resolution. Returns error string or None."""
    if workflow_id == WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID:
        return validate_score_checkpoint(
            note=note, state=state, plan=plan, force=force,
        )
    if workflow_id == WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID:
        return validate_create_plan(
            note=note, plan=plan, force=force,
        )
    # Other workflow items (import-scores, communicate-score) pass through
    return None


def has_workflow_validation(workflow_id: str) -> bool:
    """Return True if this workflow ID has hard enforcement."""
    return workflow_id in _WORKFLOW_VALIDATORS
```

```python
    # Handle synthetic IDs (triage::*, workflow::*, subjective::*) directly
    synthetic_ids, real_patterns = _resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns)
    if synthetic_ids:
        plan = load_plan()
        force = getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)

        # Validate triage dependency chain
        blocked_map = _blocked_triage_stages(plan)
        for sid in synthetic_ids:
            if sid in blocked_map:
                deps = ", ".join(b.replace("triage::", "") for b in blocked_map[sid])
                print(colorize(f"  Cannot resolve {sid} — blocked by: {deps}", "red"))
                print(colorize("  Complete those stages first, or use --force-resolve to override.", "dim"))
                if not force:
                    return

        # Validate workflow items have real content
        workflow_ids = [sid for sid in synthetic_ids if sid.startswith("workflow::")]
        if workflow_ids:
            from desloppify.app.commands.plan.workflow_validation import (
                has_workflow_validation,
                validate_workflow_item,
            )
            runtime = command_runtime(args)
            state_data = runtime.state

            for wid in workflow_ids:
                if not has_workflow_validation(wid):
                    continue
                if force:
                    # --force-resolve: still require a note explaining the skip
                    if not note or len(note.strip()) < 50:
                        print(colorize(
                            f"  --force-resolve for {wid} still requires --note "
                            f"(min 50 chars) explaining why you're skipping validation.",
                            "red",
                        ))
                        return
                    print(colorize(
                        f"  WARNING: Force-resolving {wid} — skipping content validation.",
                        "yellow",
                    ))
                    append_log_entry(
                        plan, "force_resolve_workflow",
                        issue_ids=[wid], actor="user",
                        note=note,
                        detail={"forced": True},
                    )
                    continue
                error = validate_workflow_item(
                    wid, note=note, state=state_data, plan=plan,
                )
                if error:
                    for line in error.split("\n"):
                        print(colorize(f"  {line}", "red" if not line.startswith("  ") else "dim"))
                    print(colorize(
                        f"  Use --force-resolve --note '...' to bypass (logged prominently).",
                        "dim",
                    ))
                    return

        purge_ids(plan, synthetic_ids)
        append_log_entry(
            plan, "done", issue_ids=synthetic_ids, actor="user", note=note,
        )
        save_plan(plan)
        for sid in synthetic_ids:
            print(colorize(f"  Resolved: {sid}", "green"))
        if not real_patterns:
            return
        # Continue with remaining real patterns
        patterns = real_patterns
        args.patterns = patterns
```

Key changes:
- Import `command_runtime` is already available (used later at line 538)
- Load state only when workflow IDs are present (lazy)
- Each workflow ID is validated via `validate_workflow_item()`
- `--force-resolve` still works but requires a note and logs prominently with a `force_resolve_workflow` log entry type
- Errors are printed with the same `colorize` pattern used everywhere

### Sequencing and Dependencies

1. Create `workflow_validation.py` first (no dependencies on other changes).
2. Modify `override_handlers.py` to call the new validation (depends on step 1).
3. Update `synthetic_workflow.py` primary_command hints (independent of steps 1-2, can be done in parallel).
4. Update `parser_groups_plan_impl.py` help text (independent).

### Design Decisions and Tradeoffs

**Why a separate module instead of inline validation?** The triage pattern puts validation in `_stage_validation.py` -- following that pattern keeps the code organized and testable. `override_handlers.py` is already 633 lines; adding 150+ lines of validation logic inline would push it past maintainability.

**Why validate against state data rather than just note length?** The whole point is preventing agents from generating plausible-sounding but content-free notes. Requiring the actual strict score number (e.g., "72.3") means the agent had to at least read the scorecard. Requiring cluster names means clusters had to actually exist.

**Why not require explicit `--attest` for workflow items?** The `--confirm` flag already generates attestation from the note. The problem was not the attestation mechanism but the zero validation on the note content. Adding a separate attestation requirement would be friction without value -- the note content validation is the real enforcement.

**Why still allow `--force-resolve`?** There are legitimate cases (e.g., a plan was already manually organized before the workflow item was injected). But the force path is logged with a distinct `force_resolve_workflow` log entry type and requires a note, so it leaves an audit trail.

**Score matching strategy**: The validation accepts both `str(int(round(strict)))` and the 1-decimal form. So if strict is 72.3, both "72" and "72.3" pass. This avoids false rejections when an agent rounds differently.

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` - Core integration point: synthetic ID handling block (lines 500-523)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic_workflow.py` - Update primary_command hints to show required content format
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_plan_impl.py` - Update --force-resolve help text
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation.py` - Reference pattern: existing enforcement model to follow
